<div class='question question'>

Multiply $34$ by $17$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
        &    3    &    4 \\
\times    &    1    &    7 \\
\hline
    2    &    3    &    8 \\
    3    &    4    &    0 \\
\hline
    5    &    7    &    8
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$578$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
        &    3    &    4 \\
\times    &    1    &    7 \\
\hline

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-A1E17D61-21D9-42A4-9375-6EFCBEE2EB18'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Work out $2.35 \times 1.8$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Let's first multiply the numbers ignoring the decimal points.


$
\begin {array}{cccccccccccccc}
         &     2     &     3     &     5 \\
\times   &           &     1     &     8 \\
\hline 
   1     &     8     &     8     &     0 \\
   2     &     3     &     5     &     0 \\
\hline 
   4     &     2     &     3     &     0
\end {array}
$

Between the two numbers, the total number of digits after the decimal point is three.

Let's account for the decimal point now. 

$= 4.23$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4.23$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_multiply_when_decimal_points_note

$
\begin {array}{cccccccccccccc}
         &     2     &     3     &     5 \\
\times   &           &     1     &     8 \\
\hline 
   1     &     8     &     8     &     0 \\
   2     &     3     &     5     &     0 \\
\hline 
   4     &     2     &     3     &     0
\end {array}
$

Between the two numbers, the total number of digits after the decimal point is three.

Let's account for the decimal point now. 

$= 4.23$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4.23$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-A9D3F12F-85C8-4CF8-8225-C1BB3ABCA272'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Work out $2.42 \times 1.6$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Let's first multiply the numbers ignoring the decimal points.


$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
            &   2   &   4    &    2 \\
\times      &       &   1    &    6 \\
\hline  
1           &   4   &   5    &    2 \\ 
2           &   4   &   2    &    0 \\
\hline  
3           &   8   &   7    &    2
\end{array}
$

Between the two numbers, the total number of digits after the decimal point is three.

Let's account for the decimal point now.

$= 3.872$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3.872$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_multiply_when_decimal_points_note

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
            &   2   &   4    &    2 \\
\times      &       &   1    &    6 \\
\hline  
1           &   4   &   5    &    2 \\ 
2           &   4   &   2    &    0 \\
\hline  
3           &   8   &   7    &    2
\end{array}
$

Between the two numbers, the total number of digits after the decimal point is three.

Let's account for the decimal point now.

$= 3.872$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3.872$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

Work out $573 \times 300$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let's first multiply the numbers ignoring the zeroes.

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
            &       5       & 7     & 3     \\
{}\times    &               &       & 3     \\
\hline
        1   &       7       & 1     & 9     
\end{array}
$

Let's account for the two zeroes now.

$= 171900$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$171900$

</div>
</div>

$

Let's account for the two zeroes now.

$= 171900$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-ED448CD6-9057-4F77-BD62-61F1272214D8'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Work out $0.03 \times 3200$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.03 \times 3200 \\\\
&= \dfrac {3} {100} \times 3200 \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 3200} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 32\cancel{00}} {1\cancel{00}} \\\\
&= 3 \times 32 \\\\
&= 96
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$96$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.03 \times 3200 \\\\
&= \dfrac {3} {100} \times 3200 \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 3200} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 32\cancel{00}} {1\cancel{00}} \\\\
&= 3 \times 32 \\\\
&= 96
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$96$
</code></pre>

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let's first multiply the numbers ignoring the zeroes.

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
        &   5   &   4   &   7 \\
\times  &       &       &   3 \\
\hline 
    1   &   6   &   4   &   1
\end{array}
$

Let's account for the three zeroes now.

$= 1641000$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1641000$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
        &   5   &   4   &   7 \\
\times  &       &       &   3 \\
\hline 
    1   &   6   &   4   &   1
\end{array}
$

Let's account for the three zeroes now.

$= 1641000$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
